## Academic Strategy - GPA Target and Transfer Plan - Formal

- **Minimum Required for Transfer to Mathematics and Computer Science Second Semester:** GPA > 2.4 - University Regulation

- **Why High GPA:** High GPA required for transfer to Mathematics and Computer Science specialization and for subsequent transfer to United States universities - MIT, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, UC Berkeley, Georgia Tech require excellent academic record

### CHEM 101: General Chemistry I - Lecture - كيمياء عامة (1) محاضرة - 3 Credits - Lecture

**Course Description:** General Chemistry I covering atomic structure, chemical bonding, organic chemistry fundamentals, and states of matter. Foundation for understanding chemical principles essential for physics and materials science.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Understand atomic orbitals and hybridization with quantum mechanical basis
2. Master types of chemical bonds and electron displacement effects
3. Understand isomerism and organic chemistry - alkanes, alkenes, alkynes
4. Master gaseous state, liquids, and solutions with thermodynamic principles

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 10 Components:**

- [ ] **Chapter 1 - Atomic Orbitals and Hybridization - Quantum numbers, s, p, d, f orbitals, sp, sp2, sp3 hybridization, molecular orbital theory**
- [ ] **Chapter 2 - Types of Chemical Bonds - Ionic, covalent, coordinate, metallic, hydrogen bonding, electronegativity, bond energy**
- [ ] **Chapter 3 - Electron Displacement (Inductive Effect) - Inductive effect, resonance, hyperconjugation, electron withdrawing and donating groups**
- [ ] **Chapter 4 - Isomerism and Formulae - Structural isomerism, geometrical isomerism, optical isomerism, conformational analysis, IUPAC nomenclature**
- [ ] **Chapter 5 - Alkanes - Structure, nomenclature, physical properties, chemical reactions - halogenation, combustion, cracking**
- [ ] **Chapter 6 - Alkenes - Structure, nomenclature, physical properties, chemical reactions - addition reactions, Markovnikov rule, polymerization**
- [ ] **Chapter 7 - Alkynes - Structure, nomenclature, physical properties, chemical reactions - acidity of acetylene, addition reactions**
- [ ] **Chapter 8 - The Gaseous State - Gas laws, ideal gas equation, kinetic theory, van der Waals equation, diffusion, effusion**
- [ ] **Chapter 9 - Liquids - Properties of liquids, vapor pressure, surface tension, viscosity, intermolecular forces, liquid crystals**
- [ ] **Chapter 10 - Solutions - Types of solutions, concentration units, colligative properties, solubility, Henry's law, Raoult's law**

**Selected Bibliography - High-Value Academic References - Professor Approved:**

|--------|----------------------|----------------------------------|----------|

| 3 | Chemistry: The Central Science - Brown, Lemay, Bursten - 14th Edition - Pearson | Comprehensive general chemistry with problem-solving focus | - [ ] |

### CHEM 103: Qualitative Analysis Lab - General Chemistry I Laboratory - عملي كيمياء عامة (1) - 1 Credits - Laboratory

**Course Description:** Qualitative analysis laboratory covering systematic analysis of acid radicals and basic radicals through group separation and identification. Essential practical skills for chemical analysis.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Master systematic qualitative analysis scheme for acid and basic radicals
2. Understand group separation principles based on solubility products and complex formation
3. Develop practical laboratory skills - precipitation, filtration, confirmatory tests
4. Understand chemical reactions behind qualitative analysis - safety and precision

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 9 Components:**

- [ ] **Experiment 1: Acid Radicals Group I (Dilute HCl) - Carbonate, Bicarbonate, Sulfide, Sulfite, Thiosulfate, Nitrite - Dilute HCl group - CO2 evolution, sulfide tests, sulfite, thiosulfate, nitrite**
- [ ] **Experiment 2: Acid Radicals Group II (Concentrated H2SO4) - Chloride, Bromide, Iodide, Nitrate - Concentrated H2SO4 group - Halide tests, nitrate brown ring test**
- [ ] **Experiment 3: Acid Radicals Group III (Barium Chloride) - Sulfate, Phosphate, Borate - Barium chloride group - Sulfate BaSO4 precipitation, phosphate, borate tests**
- [ ] **Experiment 4: Basic Radicals Group I - Lead, Silver, Mercury(I) - Pb2+, Ag+, Hg2(2+) - Group I - Chloride precipitation, separation**
- [ ] **Experiment 5: Basic Radicals Group II - Copper, Mercury(II), Bismuth, Cadmium - Cu2+, Hg2+, Bi3+, Cd2+ - Group II - Sulfide precipitation in acidic medium**
- [ ] **Experiment 6: Basic Radicals Group III - Iron(II), Iron(III), Aluminum, Chromium - Fe2+, Fe3+, Al3+, Cr3+ - Group III - Hydroxide precipitation with ammonium chloride and ammonium hydroxide**
- [ ] **Experiment 7: Basic Radicals Group IV - Zinc, Manganese, Cobalt, Nickel - Zn2+, Mn2+, Co2+, Ni2+ - Group IV - Sulfide precipitation in ammoniacal medium**
- [ ] **Experiment 8: Basic Radicals Group V - Calcium, Strontium, Barium - Ca2+, Sr2+, Ba2+ - Group V - Carbonate precipitation**
- [ ] **Experiment 9: Basic Radicals Group VI - Magnesium, Potassium, Sodium, Ammonium - Mg2+, K+, Na+, NH4+ - Group VI - Soluble group - flame tests, confirmatory tests**

### PHYS 101: General Physics I - Electricity and Magnetism - Lecture - فيزياء عامة (1) محاضرة - كهربية ومغناطيسية - 3 Credits - Lecture

**Course Description:** General Physics I - Electricity and Magnetism covering electric fields, Gauss's law, electric potential, capacitors, current, direct current circuits, magnetic fields, sources of magnetic fields, and Faraday's law. Foundation for electromagnetic theory.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Master electric field concept, Coulomb's law, superposition, electric field calculations for continuous charge distributions
2. Understand Gauss's law - flux, applications to symmetric charge distributions - spherical, cylindrical, planar
3. Master electric potential - potential difference, potential of point charges and continuous distributions, equipotential surfaces
4. Understand capacitors, capacitance, dielectrics, energy storage in electric field
5. Master current, resistance, Ohm's law, resistivity, temperature dependence, electrical power
6. Understand direct current circuits - Kirchhoff's laws, RC circuits, charging and discharging
7. Master magnetic field - magnetic force on charges and currents, motion of charges in magnetic field
8. Understand sources of magnetic field - Biot-Savart law, Ampere's law, applications
9. Master Faraday's law - induced EMF, Lenz's law, motional EMF, induced electric fields

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 9 Components:**

- [ ] **Chapter 23 - Electric Field - Electric charges, Coulomb's law, electric field definition, electric field of continuous charge distributions - line, surface, volume, motion of charged particle in electric field**
- [ ] **Chapter 24 - Gauss's Law - Electric flux, Gauss's law statement and proof, applications - spherical symmetry, cylindrical symmetry, planar symmetry, conductors in electrostatic equilibrium**
- [ ] **Chapter 25 - Electric Potential - Potential difference, potential of point charges, potential of continuous distributions, obtaining electric field from potential, equipotential surfaces, potential of charged conductor**
- [ ] **Chapter 26 - Capacitors and Dielectrics - Definition of capacitance, calculating capacitance - parallel plate, cylindrical, spherical, capacitors in series and parallel, energy stored, capacitors with dielectrics, molecular model of dielectrics**
- [ ] **Chapter 27 - Current and Resistance - Electric current, current density, resistance and resistivity, Ohm's law, model for electrical conduction, resistance and temperature, electrical power, superconductors**
- [ ] **Chapter 28 - Direct Current Circuits - EMF, resistors in series and parallel, Kirchhoff's rules, RC circuits - charging and discharging, multimeter, household wiring and electrical safety**
- [ ] **Chapter 29 - Magnetic Field - Magnetic field definition, magnetic force on charged particle, motion of charged particle in magnetic field - cyclotron, velocity selector, magnetic force on current-carrying conductor, torque on current loop**
- [ ] **Chapter 30 - Sources of the Magnetic Field - Biot-Savart law - magnetic field of current distributions, magnetic force between parallel conductors, Ampere's law, magnetic field of solenoid, magnetic flux, Gauss's law in magnetism, displacement current, magnetism in matter**
- [ ] **Chapter 31 - Faraday's Law - Faraday's law of induction - induced EMF, motional EMF, Lenz's law, induced EMF and electric fields, generators and motors, eddy currents**

### PHYS 101 LAB: General Physics I - Electricity Lab - فيزياء عامة (1) عملي - معمل الكهربية - 1 Credits - Laboratory

**Course Description:** Electricity laboratory covering meter bridge, leakage method, Joule's law, and verification of Kirchhoff's law. Practical understanding of electrical measurements and circuit laws.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Master meter bridge principle and applications for resistance measurement - Wheatstone bridge concept
2. Understand leakage method for high resistance measurement and capacitor leakage
3. Verify Joule's law - heat produced by electric current - electrical equivalent of heat
4. Verify Kirchhoff's laws - current law and voltage law - practical circuit analysis

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 4 Components:**

- [ ] **Experiment 1: Meter Bridge - Principle of Wheatstone bridge, meter bridge construction, determination of unknown resistance, resistivity of material, end corrections**
- [ ] **Experiment 2: Leakage Method - High resistance measurement by leakage method, capacitor discharge, time constant, leakage resistance**
- [ ] **Experiment 3: Joule's Law - Verification of Joule's law - heat produced by current, electrical equivalent of heat, calorimeter, energy conversion**
- [ ] **Experiment 4: Verification of Kirchhoff's Law - Kirchhoff's current law (KCL) and voltage law (KVL) verification, series and parallel circuits, practical measurements with ammeter and voltmeter**

### PHYS 103: General Physics II - Mechanics - Lecture - فيزياء عامة (2) محاضرة - ميكانيكا - 3 Credits - Lecture

**Course Description:** General Physics II - Mechanics covering physics and measurement, motion in one and two dimensions, vectors, laws of motion, circular motion, energy, potential energy, linear momentum and collisions, rotation of rigid objects, angular momentum, static equilibrium and elasticity.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Master physics and measurement - standards, units, dimensional analysis, significant figures, error analysis
2. Understand motion in one dimension - displacement, velocity, acceleration, kinematic equations, free fall
3. Master vectors - vector addition, components, unit vectors, scalar and vector products
4. Understand motion in two dimensions - projectile motion, uniform circular motion, tangential and radial acceleration
5. Master laws of motion - Newton's three laws, inertial frames, friction, drag forces, applications
6. Understand circular motion and applications - uniform and non-uniform circular motion, centripetal force, applications
7. Master energy and energy transfer - work, work-energy theorem, power, work done by varying forces
8. Understand potential energy - conservative and non-conservative forces, potential energy functions, energy diagrams, equilibrium
9. Master linear momentum and collisions - momentum, impulse, conservation of momentum, collisions - elastic and inelastic, center of mass
10. Understand rotation of rigid object about fixed axis - angular displacement, velocity, acceleration, rotational kinematics, torque, moment of inertia, rotational work and energy
11. Master angular momentum - angular momentum of particle and rigid object, conservation of angular momentum, precession
12. Understand static equilibrium and elasticity - conditions for equilibrium, center of gravity, elasticity - Young's modulus, shear modulus, bulk modulus

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 12 Components:**

- [ ] **Chapter 1: Physics and Measurement - Standards of length, mass, time, matter and model building, dimensional analysis, conversion of units, estimates and order-of-magnitude calculations, significant figures**
- [ ] **Chapter 2: Motion in One Dimension - Position, velocity, speed, acceleration, motion diagrams, particle under constant velocity and constant acceleration, free fall, kinematic equations**
- [ ] **Chapter 3: Vectors - Coordinate systems, vector and scalar quantities, properties of vectors - equality, addition, negative, subtraction, multiplication by scalar, components and unit vectors, scalar product, vector product**
- [ ] **Chapter 4: Motion in Two Dimensions - Position, velocity, acceleration vectors, two-dimensional motion with constant acceleration, projectile motion - horizontal range, maximum height, uniform circular motion, tangential and radial acceleration, relative velocity**
- [ ] **Chapter 5: Laws of Motion - Concept of force, Newton's first law, second law - force and acceleration, mass, gravitational force and weight, Newton's third law, applications - particle in equilibrium and under net force, friction - static and kinetic, drag forces**
- [ ] **Chapter 6: Circular Motion and Other Applications of Newton's Laws - Extending particle in uniform circular motion model, non-uniform circular motion, motion in accelerated frames - fictitious forces, motion in presence of resistive forces - terminal velocity**
- [ ] **Chapter 7: Energy and Energy Transfer - Systems, work done by constant force, work done by varying force - spring force, work-kinetic energy theorem, power - instantaneous and average, work done by non-constant forces**
- [ ] **Chapter 8: Potential Energy - Analysis model - particle in equilibrium, potential energy of system, conservative and non-conservative forces, conservative forces and potential energy, energy diagrams and equilibrium of system**
- [ ] **Chapter 9: Linear Momentum and Collisions - Linear momentum, analysis model - isolated system - conservation of momentum, analysis model - non-isolated system - impulse and momentum, collisions in one dimension - elastic and inelastic, collisions in two dimensions, center of mass, systems of many particles**
- [ ] **Chapter 10: Rotation of Rigid Object About Fixed Axis - Angular position, velocity, acceleration, analysis model - rigid object under constant angular acceleration, angular and translational quantities, rotational kinetic energy, moment of inertia calculation, torque, analysis model - rigid object under net torque, energy considerations in rotational motion, rolling motion**
- [ ] **Chapter 11: Angular Momentum - Angular momentum of particle, analysis model - non-isolated system - angular momentum, angular momentum of rigid object, analysis model - isolated system - conservation of angular momentum, motion of spinning top and gyroscope**
- [ ] **Chapter 12: Static Equilibrium and Elasticity - Analysis model - rigid object in equilibrium, more on center of gravity, examples of rigid objects in static equilibrium, elastic properties of solids - Young's modulus, shear modulus, bulk modulus**

### PHYS 103 TUT: General Physics II - Mechanics Tutorial - فيزياء عامة (2) تمارين - ميكانيكا تمارين - 1 Credits - Tutorial

**Course Description:** Mechanics tutorial covering problem-solving for all mechanics topics. Focus on developing problem-solving skills for achieving high GPA 3.5 to 4.0.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Master problem-solving techniques for physics and measurement - error analysis, dimensional analysis
2. Develop skills for motion in one dimension - kinematic equation applications
3. Master vector problems - addition, components, products
4. Develop projectile motion and circular motion problem-solving
5. Master Newton's laws applications - free body diagrams, friction problems
6. Develop energy and momentum problem-solving - work-energy, conservation laws
7. Master rotational motion problems - moment of inertia, torque, angular momentum
8. Prepare for exams with high GPA target - 3.5 to 4.0

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 12 Components:**

- [ ] **Tutorial 1: Physics and Measurement - Problems - Units, dimensional analysis, significant figures, error propagation, estimation problems - GPA focused**
- [ ] **Tutorial 2: Motion in One Dimension - Problems - Kinematic equations, free fall, graphical analysis, calculus-based problems**
- [ ] **Tutorial 3: Vectors - Problems - Vector addition, components, unit vectors, scalar and vector products - applications**
- [ ] **Tutorial 4: Motion in Two Dimensions - Problems - Projectile motion - range, height, time of flight, circular motion, relative velocity**
- [ ] **Tutorial 5: Laws of Motion - Problems - Newton's laws applications, free body diagrams, inclined planes, pulleys, friction - static and kinetic**
- [ ] **Tutorial 6: Circular Motion and Applications - Problems - Uniform and non-uniform circular motion, centripetal force, banked curves, conical pendulum**
- [ ] **Tutorial 7: Energy and Energy Transfer - Problems - Work, work-energy theorem, power, work by varying forces - spring, gravitational**
- [ ] **Tutorial 8: Potential Energy - Problems - Conservative forces, potential energy functions, energy diagrams, equilibrium points**
- [ ] **Tutorial 9: Linear Momentum and Collisions - Problems - Conservation of momentum, impulse, elastic and inelastic collisions, center of mass, rocket propulsion**
- [ ] **Tutorial 10: Rotation of Rigid Object About Fixed Axis - Problems - Rotational kinematics, moment of inertia, torque, rotational dynamics, rolling motion, work-energy in rotation**
- [ ] **Tutorial 11: Angular Momentum - Problems - Angular momentum conservation, spinning top, gyroscope, Kepler's laws**
- [ ] **Tutorial 12: Static Equilibrium and Elasticity - Problems - Equilibrium conditions, center of gravity, elasticity - stress, strain, moduli**

### MATH 101: Calculus I and Analytic Geometry - Lecture - تفاضل وتكامل (1) محاضرة - 3 Credits - Lecture

**Course Description:** Calculus I and Analytic Geometry covering differentiation, integration, rectangular Cartesian coordinates, straight lines, pairs of straight lines, general equation of second degree, and conic sections. Foundation for all physics and mathematics.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Master differentiation - limits, continuity, derivatives, rules, applications - maxima, minima, related rates
2. Master integration - indefinite and definite integrals, techniques - substitution, by parts, partial fractions
3. Understand rectangular Cartesian coordinates and straight line - distance formula, section formula, straight line equations
4. Master pairs of straight lines and general equation of second degree - homogeneous equations, angle between lines, general second degree
5. Master conic sections - circle, parabola, ellipse, hyperbola - equations, properties, applications

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 5 Components:**

- [ ] **Chapter 1: Differentiation - Limits, continuity, definition of derivative, rules of differentiation - product, quotient, chain rule, differentiation of implicit functions, parametric functions, logarithmic differentiation, higher order derivatives, applications - maxima and minima, mean value theorems - Rolle's, Lagrange's, Cauchy's, L'Hospital rule, related rates**
- [ ] **Chapter 2: Integration - Indefinite integrals, definite integrals, Fundamental theorem of calculus, techniques of integration - substitution, integration by parts, partial fractions, integration of rational, irrational, trigonometric functions, definite integrals - properties, applications - area, volume, length of curves, surface area**
- [ ] **Chapter 3: Rectangular Cartesian Coordinates and Straight Line - Cartesian coordinates, distance formula, section formula - internal and external division, area of triangle, locus, straight line - slope, intercept form, point-slope form, two-point form, general form, angle between lines, distance of point from line, equations of bisectors**
- [ ] **Chapter 4: Pairs of Straight Lines and General Equation of Second Degree - Pair of straight lines through origin - homogeneous equation of second degree, angle between lines, condition for perpendicular and coincident lines, general equation of second degree - pair of straight lines, condition for pair of straight lines, point of intersection, angle between lines**
- [ ] **Chapter 5: Conic Sections - Circle - equation, center, radius, general equation, tangent, normal, chord, parabola - standard equations, properties, parametric equations, tangent, normal, ellipse - standard equations, properties, eccentricity, directrix, tangent, normal, hyperbola - standard equations, properties, asymptotes, rectangular hyperbola, general conic - classification, reduction to standard forms**

### ASU 101: Societal Issues - Lecture - قضايا مجتمعية - محاضرة - University Requirement - 1 Credits - Lecture - University Requirement

**Course Description:** University requirement covering societal issues including greenhouse gases, global warming, climate change effects in Egypt, water resources, food security, energy, sustainable development, transparency, integrity, and anti-corruption.

4. Understand energy and sustainable development - renewable energy, sustainable development goals
5. Master transparency, integrity, anti-corruption - concepts, mechanisms, role in development

- [ ] **Chapter 3: Water Resources and Food Security - Water resources in Egypt - Nile, groundwater, desalination, water scarcity, water management, food security - challenges, agriculture, population growth, sustainable agriculture, virtual water**

### SAFS 101: Safety and Security - Safety in Laboratories - Lecture - أمن وسلامة - محاضرة - University Requirement - 1 Credits - Lecture - University Requirement

**Course Description:** University requirement covering safety in laboratories including safety vs security, risk, hazard, incident, accident, JHA/JSA, APP, OSHA, risk management, ergonomic safety, ladder safety, electrical safety, fire safety, chemical safety, MSDS, lab rules, PPE, biosafety levels.

**Learning Objectives - GPA 4.0 Focused:**

1. Understand safety vs security, risk, hazard, incident, accident - definitions and differences
2. Master JHA/JSA, APP, OSHA, risk management - job hazard analysis, accident prevention plan
3. Understand ergonomic safety and ladder safety - ergonomics principles, ladder types, safe use
4. Master electrical safety - electrical hazards, lockout/tagout, safe practices
5. Understand fire safety and fire classes - fire triangle, fire classes A, B, C, D, K, extinguishers, evacuation
6. Master chemical safety and MSDS - chemical hazards, MSDS sheets, labeling, storage, handling
7. Understand lab rules, PPE, biosafety levels - laboratory rules, personal protective equipment, biosafety levels BSL-1 to BSL-4

**Prerequisites:** High school physics, chemistry, mathematics - calculus basics. For university requirements - no prerequisites.

**Detailed Chapters and Experiments - 7 Components:**

- [ ] **Chapter 1: Safety vs Security, Risk, Hazard, Incident, Accident - Safety definition, security definition, difference, risk definition - risk assessment, hazard definition - types of hazards, incident definition - near miss, accident definition - accident investigation**
- [ ] **Chapter 2: JHA/JSA, APP, OSHA, Risk Management - Job Hazard Analysis (JHA) / Job Safety Analysis (JSA) - steps, Accident Prevention Plan (APP) - components, Occupational Safety and Health Administration (OSHA) - standards, regulations, Risk Management - risk identification, evaluation, control, hierarchy of controls**
- [ ] **Chapter 3: Ergonomic Safety and Ladder Safety - Ergonomic Safety - definition, musculoskeletal disorders, ergonomic principles, workstation design, Ladder Safety - ladder types - step, extension, fixed, ladder hazards, safe use, inspection**
- [ ] **Chapter 4: Electrical Safety - Electrical hazards - shock, arc flash, fire, electrical safety principles - grounding, insulation, lockout/tagout (LOTO), electrical PPE, safe work practices, first aid for electrical shock**
- [ ] **Chapter 5: Fire Safety and Fire Classes - Fire triangle and tetrahedron, fire classes - Class A - ordinary combustibles, Class B - flammable liquids, Class C - electrical, Class D - combustible metals, Class K - cooking oils, fire extinguishers - types, selection, use - PASS method, fire evacuation, fire prevention**
- [ ] **Chapter 6: Chemical Safety and MSDS - Chemical hazards - toxicity, corrosivity, flammability, reactivity, Material Safety Data Sheets (MSDS) / Safety Data Sheets (SDS) - 16 sections, chemical labeling - GHS, NFPA diamond, chemical storage - compatibility, handling, spill response**
- [ ] **Chapter 7: Lab Rules, PPE and Biosafety Levels - Laboratory Rules - general rules, specific rules for chemistry, physics, biology labs, Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) - types - gloves, goggles, lab coat, respirators, selection, use, Biosafety Levels - BSL-1, BSL-2, BSL-3, BSL-4 - requirements, practices, facilities**

## Introduction - Formal Academic Statement

This curriculum represents a comprehensive doctoral preparatory program designed for an exceptional individual pursuing a PhD in Computer Science with focus on Scalable Foundation Models and Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning and AI Systems, and founding the world's leading artificial intelligence company in this domain.

The curriculum consists of thirty-eight focused interdisciplinary programs covering foundational computer science, mathematics, artificial intelligence, machine learning, systems, and specialized areas critical for scalable foundation models and multi-agent systems. Each program includes extraordinary research projects built from first principles, selected high-value academic references, and verified learning resources.

The curriculum is structured to provide sustainable competitive advantage through depth in areas that constitute moat for a leading artificial intelligence company: scalable training, multi-agent reinforcement learning, machine learning systems, efficient artificial intelligence, data engineering, and safety and alignment.

## Detailed Program Specifications - Professor-Level Formal


### Program 1: Computer Science

**Program Description:** Foundational computer science covering programming paradigms, data structures, algorithms, and networking. Provides rigorous systems understanding essential for artificial intelligence systems engineering.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** KareemLang: Complete Systems Programming Language with Concurrent Garbage Collection and Package Registry from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 3: Mathematics

**Program Description:** Rigorous mathematics including real analysis, linear algebra, abstract algebra, topology, and complex analysis. Develops mathematical maturity required for doctoral research in computer science.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Symbolic Mathematics Engine with Automated Theorem Proving and Lean 4 Integration from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

| 3 | Abstract Algebra - Dummit and Foote - Wiley | Comprehensive algebra - groups, rings, fields |

### Program 4: Physics for AI Systems

**Program Description:** Physics principles relevant to artificial intelligence including classical mechanics, thermodynamics, statistical mechanics, and their connections to energy-based models and diffusion models.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Unified Physics Simulation Engine with Real-Time Visualization - Classical, Electromagnetic, Thermodynamic, Quantum

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 5: Artificial Intelligence

**Program Description:** Core artificial intelligence including search, knowledge representation, reasoning, planning, and decision-theoretic reasoning. Foundation for general intelligence.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** General Game-Playing Agent with Search, Knowledge Representation, and Decision-Theoretic Reasoning from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 7: Machine Learning

**Program Description:** Machine learning theory and algorithms including supervised, unsupervised, ensemble methods, and evaluation. Implementation from first principles without external libraries.

**Learning Objectives:**

### Program 10: Computer Architecture and Accelerators

**Program Description:** Computer architecture including RISC-V processor design, cache hierarchy, and custom accelerators for artificial intelligence including systolic arrays.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** RISC-V Processor with Cache Hierarchy and Custom Systolic-Array AI Accelerator in Verilog - FPGA Implementation

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 12: Theory of Computation and Complexity

**Program Description:** Theory of computation including automata, computability, complexity classes, approximation algorithms, and randomized algorithms.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Automated Complexity Analyzer and Approximation Algorithm Generator with Formal Proofs

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 14: Optimization Theory

**Program Description:** Optimization including convex, non-convex, stochastic, and distributed optimization. Core for training foundation models.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** High-Performance Optimization Solver Comparable to Gurobi - Convex, Non-Convex, Integer, Distributed from Scratch

The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. ### Program 18: Deep Learning

**Program Description:** Deep learning foundations, architectures, optimization, regularization, and implementation from first principles with custom CUDA kernels.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Deep Learning Framework Comparable to PyTorch from Scratch with Custom CUDA Kernels

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 20: Natural Language Processing

**Program Description:** Natural language processing including linguistics, transformer architectures, language modeling, and Arabic-centric natural language processing.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Arabic-Centric Transformer Training Pipeline with Custom Tokenizer from Scratch - Modern Standard Arabic and Egyptian Dialect

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 21: Generative AI and Large Language Models

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.

The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. ### Program 22: Reinforcement Learning and Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning

**Program Description:** Advanced reinforcement learning including deep reinforcement learning, multi-agent reinforcement learning, self-play, and emergent multi-agent communication.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Multi-Agent Civilization Simulation with One Hundred Learning Agents - Multi-Agent Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient, QMIX, Emergent Cooperation

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 25: Cryptography and Secure AI Systems

**Program Description:** Modern cryptography including post-quantum cryptography, zero-knowledge proofs, and secure multi-party computation for privacy-preserving machine learning.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Post-Quantum Secure Messaging Protocol with Zero-Knowledge Proofs and Secure Multi-Party Computation from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 30: Game Theory, Mechanism Design, and Multi-Agent Systems

**Program Description:** Game theory including Nash equilibrium, mechanism design, auctions, and multi-agent systems for artificial intelligence agent economies.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Game-Theoretic Auction Mechanism for AI Agents Trading Resources with Nash Equilibrium Proofs

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 33: Cognitive Science and Decision Making

**Program Description:** Cognitive science including memory, decision making, dual-process theory, and cognitive architectures.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Cognitive Architecture Simulating Human Memory, Decision Making, and Embodied Cognition

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 35: AI Safety, Alignment, and Interpretability

**Program Description:** Artificial intelligence safety including reinforcement learning from human feedback, direct preference optimization, constitutional artificial intelligence, mechanistic interpretability, and safety evaluation.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Complete Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback and Constitutional AI Pipeline from Scratch with Mechanistic Interpretability Dashboard

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review

### Program 36: Data Engineering and Data-Centric AI for Foundation Models

**Program Description:** Data engineering for foundation models including large-scale pipelines for one hundred billion tokens, deduplication, filtering, synthetic data generation, governance, and scaling laws.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Large-Scale Data Pipeline for One Hundred Billion Token Foundation Model Training from Scratch

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

### Program 38: AI Systems Engineering, MLOps, LLMOps, and Evaluation

**Program Description:** Artificial intelligence systems engineering including machine learning operations, large language model operations, evaluation, monitoring, deployment at scale, and cost optimization.

**Learning Objectives:**

- Master foundational theory with mathematical rigor and formal proofs at professor level.
- Implement core algorithms and systems from first principles without external libraries.
- Develop research-level understanding suitable for doctoral studies at Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon, University of California Berkeley, and Georgia Tech.
- Build extraordinary research project demonstrating expertise beyond conventional coursework.

**Prerequisites:** Mathematics through calculus and linear algebra, programming proficiency, and completion of foundational programs as indicated in dependency structure. Programs should be followed in numerical order for optimal learning progression.

**GitHub Research Project:**

**Title:** Complete Large Language Model Operations Platform from Scratch with Evaluation, Monitoring, and Deployment

**Description:** This project requires implementation from first principles without utilizing existing libraries except standard library and basic linear algebra operations. The project is designed to demonstrate exceptional depth and breadth, serving as portfolio evidence for doctoral applications and for establishing technical credibility for founding an artificial intelligence company. The project must include formal correctness arguments, complexity analysis, comprehensive testing, and performance benchmarking.

**Repository Structure:**
- README.md: Formal project description, architecture, mathematical foundations, results, citations
- src: Source code from scratch - well-documented with formal invariants
- docs: Formal documentation with mathematical proofs, design decisions, complexity analysis, academic paper references
- tests: Comprehensive test suite including unit tests and property-based tests
- benchmarks: Performance evaluation with comparison to baselines
- papers: Related academic papers and formal literature review
